## 2026-11-06T00:44:47.600Z — T3

# FamilyBizness: Agent Handoff Brief



**Audience:** an orchestrator agent that will split this file into sub-agent assignments.

**Project:** FamilyBizness, a single-family-office (SFO) prototype built for the Hult ENT family business coursework.

**Written without inspecting the repository.** All file names, component names and data paths below are unknown. Every agent must discover the real ones with search before editing anything.



How to use this file: read "Shared standards" and "Naming model" first, because every workstream depends on them. Then pick up workstreams according to the dependency table. Each task has an ID so reports can reference it.



---



## 0. Shared standards (read before touching any UI)



These rules exist because the sidebar, floating dock and settings pages keep coming back with padding and margin mistakes. Treat them as binding for every workstream.



### 0.1 Padding and margin are different tools



| Property | What it is | Use it for |

|---|---|---|

| Padding | Space inside an element, between its border and its content. The element's background and hit area extend through it. | Breathing room inside components: nav items, cards, panels, buttons, inputs. Indentation of nested nav items. |

| Margin | Space outside an element, separating it from siblings. Transparent, never clickable. | Almost nothing. Separate siblings with `gap` on the flex or grid parent instead. |



Rules that follow from this:



1. **Indent nested nav items with `padding-left`, never `margin-left`.** Padding keeps the hover and active background spanning the full row width, so the indent shows as a shifted label inside an aligned row. Margin shifts the whole row and produces the deformed indents currently visible on Stewardship and Calendar.

2. **Space siblings with `gap` on the container.** Do not put `margin-top` on children. Child margins stack, collapse unpredictably and leak out of containers, which is the likely cause of the extra space above the logo and logo name.

3. **No leading or trailing margin at a container edge.** The first and last child of a container must not add space beyond the container's own padding.

4. **One spacing scale.** Define tokens on a 4px base (4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48). No raw pixel values in components.

5. **Fixed icon column.** Every nav row has an icon slot of identical width and height, whether or not the item has an icon, so labels align at every depth. This fixes the inconsistent icon padding in the sidebar.

6. **Indent step is a token.** Each depth adds the same `--nav-indent-step` to the label's `padding-left`. Depth is data, not hand-tuned CSS per item.

7. **Touch targets are at least 44px tall** on coarse pointers, achieved with padding.



Before changing any spacing, the agent must record for each affected element: which property currently creates the space (padding, margin, gap, or an inline style), and which element owns it. Fix the owner, not the symptom.



### 0.2 Navigation and settings semantics (Jakob's Law)



Jakob's Law says users spend most of their time on other products and expect this one to work the same way. For navigation that means following established conventions closely and leaving novelty to the content. Every decision below carries a rationale, and the agent must write those rationales into `docs/design/navigation-and-settings.md` (also feeds the About page documentation, task 29).



1. **One navigation config drives everything.** A single data structure (sections, pages, depth, icon, route, allowed roles) renders the main sidebar, the floating dock, the settings sidebar and the role filtering. Rationale: the dock currently fails to mirror the sidebar because they are separate implementations. One source of truth removes that class of bug.

2. **Left vertical sidebar for sections with sub-pages.** This is the dominant convention for dashboards and settings (GitHub, Stripe, Google account, macOS and Windows settings). Top tab rows are removed for Family Constitution, Portfolio and Scenario, and Settings.

3. **Flat, always-visible sub-pages.** Sub-pages sit indented under their section header. No dropdowns or accordions, because hidden items cost discoverability and the finance dropdown was already rejected.

4. **Grouped by user task, ordered by frequency.** Group labels are plain nouns. Most-used first, destructive or rare actions last.

5. **Current location is always visible.** Active item has a distinct background and weight, and `aria-current="page"`. The parent section header shows as the active ancestor.

6. **Semantic markup.** `nav` landmark with an `aria-label`, nested `ul` and `li`, real links for navigation (not click-handler divs), one `h1` per page, section headings in order, no skipped levels.

7. **Master-detail layout.** Sidebar on the left, content on the right with a stable max width. On narrow screens the sidebar collapses to a list view that drills into the detail page, with a clear back affordance.

8. **Collapse control.** The collapse icon has a dynamic tooltip and accessible name that switches between "Open menu" and "Close menu", and carries `aria-expanded`. In the collapsed rail every icon shows its label as a tooltip.

9. **Every page is deep-linkable.** Each sub-page has its own URL or hash route so refresh, back button and sharing work.

10. **Consistent form rows in settings.** Label, control, helper text, in that order, same spacing everywhere. Settings changes apply immediately with visible confirmation, or have an explicit Save with a dirty-state indicator. Pick one pattern and use it on every settings page.



---



## 1. Naming model



There are two names and they never mix.



| Name | Value | Where it appears |

|---|---|---|

| Internal code name | `familybizness` | GitHub repository name, package name, folder names, commit messages, the Claude project, docs for developers. Never shown in the product UI. |

| Display name | One variable: the family surname | Every user-visible string in the product. |



**N1. Single display variable.** Create one config value, `FAMILY_NAME`, holding the surname. It is read once from localStorage (with a sensible fallback), editable in Settings and on the landing page header at the top of Stewardship, and consumed everywhere through one accessor. Every other display string is derived from it, for example "{FAMILY_NAME} Family Office" for the product title, page titles and SEO metadata. The surname is undecided (candidates mentioned: Jarwal, Slavonki), so ship with a neutral placeholder and make changing it a one-field edit with an immediate, global effect.



**N2. Purge hardcoded names.** Search the whole codebase for "Golden Bird", "Goldenbird", "Project Golden Bird" and "ENT-0301". None may remain in UI strings. Remove or replace with the variable. The donor constitution is a different, already-fed document, and the new SFO is a hypothetical separate family, so the donor family's name must not leak into the interface.



**N3. Sweep for "Ghostfolio".** The name must not appear anywhere in the UI, including X-ray and Risk. Source-level attribution belongs only in the open-source licenses and credits page (task 29).



---



## 2. Roles and visibility



Roles: **Associate**, **Managing Director**, **Family Patriarch**, **Family Board**, **Legal Counsel**. The earlier titles "Associate / Family Technician" and "Managing Director / CEO" are shortened to the plain names.



| Role | Can see |

|---|---|

| Managing Director | Everything |

| Family Patriarch | Everything |

| Associate | Everything except Family Constitution and Goals |

| Family Board | Goals and the Capital and Liquidity overview only. Detailed finance pages hidden. |

| Legal Counsel | Investment screens only. Finance screens hidden. |



**R1.** Implement as an allowed-roles field in the navigation config (see 0.2 item 1). Filtering must apply to the main sidebar, floating dock, Ctrl+K search results and direct URL access. A hidden page must not be reachable by typing its route.

**R2.** Where the Family Board and Legal Counsel boundary falls exactly (which finance pages count as "overview" versus "detail", which pages count as "investment screens") is for the agent to decide from the final sidebar, and to record in the docs.

**R3.** Profile switcher: on short vertical breakpoints (mobile and tablet), the profile picker at the top becomes a swipeable slide carousel. Build it after touch handling is fixed (W1-6).



---



## 3. Target information architecture



Final names are the agent's to refine. The brief is to replace generic labels with terms a real single family office uses. "Family Business" as a header in a wealth platform does not fit and must be renamed. Structure below is binding, labels are placeholders.



1. **Stewardship** (homepage and landing page, holds the `FAMILY_NAME` field)

2. **Family Constitution** (H3 under the title: "Live connected to data lake")

   - Charter, with revisions and adoption

   - Investment Policy Statement (includes the art and culture text, see Appendix A)

   - Governance and Succession

   - Estate Planning

3. **Day-to-day Operations**

   - Calendar

   - Family Goals (moved here from Governance, includes the goals manager)

4. **Capital and Liquidity**

   - Overview (expanded liquidity-event detail)

   - Budget and Expenditure (merged with Spending Insights)

   - Investment Fees

   - Trading Analytics

   - Financial Statements (own page, accounting-software connection banner)

5. **Portfolio and Scenario** (all panes in the left sidebar, no top tabs)

   - Includes Stochastic Process Lab and Scenario Analysis as highlighted pages

   - FIRE, X-ray and Risk, and the rest of the existing panes

6. **Settings** (own left sidebar, see workstream W2-S)

7. **About** (own page)



---



## 4. Workstreams and dependencies



| ID | Workstream | Depends on | Can run in parallel with |

|---|---|---|---|

| W0 | Spacing tokens and shared standards (section 0) | none | Nothing else should start UI work before the tokens exist |

| W1 | Data correctness: goals, spending, search, touch | none | W0 |

| W2 | Navigation, IA, dock, sidebar polish, name variable | W0 | W1, W4 |

| W2-S | Settings sidebar and settings pages | W0, W2 nav config | W4, W5 |

| W3 | Role-based views and profile carousel | W1-6 (touch), W2 nav config | W4, W5 |

| W4 | Content and data population | W2 (page homes exist) | W5, W6 |

## Appendix A: Art and culture text for the Investment Policy Statement



> A family business expenditure and philanthropy may commission art, employ local makers, develop event formats and support a publishing or media venture. Each activity creates a different claim on capital. The hotel can buy decoration as an operating expense; a collection vehicle can acquire a documented work as an investment; a foundation can fund artists without claiming a financial return. Shared family taste is insufficient to classify a transaction.

>

> The family shall create and support culture as well as own financial assets. Painting, sculpture, visual arts, film, music, design, publishing, performance, digital production and architecture are eligible activities.

>

> **Art as an asset.** Art is evaluated with the discipline appropriate to a costly, heterogeneous and often illiquid asset.

## 2026-11-06T00:44:51.582Z — Codex

# FamilyBizness: Agent Handoff Brief



**Audience:** an orchestrator agent that will split this file into sub-agent assignments.

**Project:** FamilyBizness, a single-family-office (SFO) prototype built for the Hult ENT family business coursework.

**Written without inspecting the repository.** All file names, component names and data paths below are unknown. Every agent must discover the real ones with search before editing anything.



How to use this file: read "Shared standards" and "Naming model" first, because every workstream depends on them. Then pick up workstreams according to the dependency table. Each task has an ID so reports can reference it.



---



## 0. Shared standards (read before touching any UI)



These rules exist because the sidebar, floating dock and settings pages keep coming back with padding and margin mistakes. Treat them as binding for every workstream.



### 0.1 Padding and margin are different tools



| Property | What it is | Use it for |

|---|---|---|

| Padding | Space inside an element, between its border and its content. The element's background and hit area extend through it. | Breathing room inside components: nav items, cards, panels, buttons, inputs. Indentation of nested nav items. |

| Margin | Space outside an element, separating it from siblings. Transparent, never clickable. | Almost nothing. Separate siblings with `gap` on the flex or grid parent instead. |



Rules that follow from this:



1. **Indent nested nav items with `padding-left`, never `margin-left`.** Padding keeps the hover and active background spanning the full row width, so the indent shows as a shifted label inside an aligned row. Margin shifts the whole row and produces the deformed indents currently visible on Stewardship and Calendar.

2. **Space siblings with `gap` on the container.** Do not put `margin-top` on children. Child margins stack, collapse unpredictably and leak out of containers, which is the likely cause of the extra space above the logo and logo name.

3. **No leading or trailing margin at a container edge.** The first and last child of a container must not add space beyond the container's own padding.

4. **One spacing scale.** Define tokens on a 4px base (4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48). No raw pixel values in components.

5. **Fixed icon column.** Every nav row has an icon slot of identical width and height, whether or not the item has an icon, so labels align at every depth. This fixes the inconsistent icon padding in the sidebar.

6. **Indent step is a token.** Each depth adds the same `--nav-indent-step` to the label's `padding-left`. Depth is data, not hand-tuned CSS per item.

7. **Touch targets are at least 44px tall** on coarse pointers, achieved with padding.



Before changing any spacing, the agent must record for each affected element: which property currently creates the space (padding, margin, gap, or an inline style), and which element owns it. Fix the owner, not the symptom.



### 0.2 Navigation and settings semantics (Jakob's Law)



Jakob's Law says users spend most of their time on other products and expect this one to work the same way. For navigation that means following established conventions closely and leaving novelty to the content. Every decision below carries a rationale, and the agent must write those rationales into `docs/design/navigation-and-settings.md` (also feeds the About page documentation, task 29).



1. **One navigation config drives everything.** A single data structure (sections, pages, depth, icon, route, allowed roles) renders the main sidebar, the floating dock, the settings sidebar and the role filtering. Rationale: the dock currently fails to mirror the sidebar because they are separate implementations. One source of truth removes that class of bug.

2. **Left vertical sidebar for sections with sub-pages.** This is the dominant convention for dashboards and settings (GitHub, Stripe, Google account, macOS and Windows settings). Top tab rows are removed for Family Constitution, Portfolio and Scenario, and Settings.

3. **Flat, always-visible sub-pages.** Sub-pages sit indented under their section header. No dropdowns or accordions, because hidden items cost discoverability and the finance dropdown was already rejected.

4. **Grouped by user task, ordered by frequency.** Group labels are plain nouns. Most-used first, destructive or rare actions last.

5. **Current location is always visible.** Active item has a distinct background and weight, and `aria-current="page"`. The parent section header shows as the active ancestor.

6. **Semantic markup.** `nav` landmark with an `aria-label`, nested `ul` and `li`, real links for navigation (not click-handler divs), one `h1` per page, section headings in order, no skipped levels.

7. **Master-detail layout.** Sidebar on the left, content on the right with a stable max width. On narrow screens the sidebar collapses to a list view that drills into the detail page, with a clear back affordance.

8. **Collapse control.** The collapse icon has a dynamic tooltip and accessible name that switches between "Open menu" and "Close menu", and carries `aria-expanded`. In the collapsed rail every icon shows its label as a tooltip.

9. **Every page is deep-linkable.** Each sub-page has its own URL or hash route so refresh, back button and sharing work.

10. **Consistent form rows in settings.** Label, control, helper text, in that order, same spacing everywhere. Settings changes apply immediately with visible confirmation, or have an explicit Save with a dirty-state indicator. Pick one pattern and use it on every settings page.



---



## 1. Naming model



There are two names and they never mix.



| Name | Value | Where it appears |

|---|---|---|

| Internal code name | `familybizness` | GitHub repository name, package name, folder names, commit messages, the Claude project, docs for developers. Never shown in the product UI. |

| Display name | One variable: the family surname | Every user-visible string in the product. |



**N1. Single display variable.** Create one config value, `FAMILY_NAME`, holding the surname. It is read once from localStorage (with a sensible fallback), editable in Settings and on the landing page header at the top of Stewardship, and consumed everywhere through one accessor. Every other display string is derived from it, for example "{FAMILY_NAME} Family Office" for the product title, page titles and SEO metadata. The surname is undecided (candidates mentioned: Jarwal, Slavonki), so ship with a neutral placeholder and make changing it a one-field edit with an immediate, global effect.



**N2. Purge hardcoded names.** Search the whole codebase for "Golden Bird", "Goldenbird", "Project Golden Bird" and "ENT-0301". None may remain in UI strings. Remove or replace with the variable. The donor constitution is a different, already-fed document, and the new SFO is a hypothetical separate family, so the donor family's name must not leak into the interface.



**N3. Sweep for "Ghostfolio".** The name must not appear anywhere in the UI, including X-ray and Risk. Source-level attribution belongs only in the open-source licenses and credits page (task 29).

|---|---|

| Managing Director | Everything |

| Family Patriarch | Everything |

| Associate | Everything except Family Constitution and Goals |

| Family Board | Goals and the Capital and Liquidity overview only. Detailed finance pages hidden. |

| Legal Counsel | Investment screens only. Finance screens hidden. |

## Appendix A: Art and culture text for the Investment Policy Statement



> A family business expenditure and philanthropy may commission art, employ local makers, develop event formats and support a publishing or media venture. Each activity creates a different claim on capital. The hotel can buy decoration as an operating expense; a collection vehicle can acquire a documented work as an investment; a foundation can fund artists without claiming a financial return. Shared family taste is insufficient to classify a transaction.

>

> The family shall create and support culture as well as own financial assets. Painting, sculpture, visual arts, film, music, design, publishing, performance, digital production and architecture are eligible activities.

>

> **Art as an asset.** Art is evaluated with the discipline appropriate to a costly, heterogeneous and often illiquid asset.

## 2026-11-06T00:45:26.638Z — Codex

# FamilyBizness: Agent Handoff Brief



**Audience:** an orchestrator agent that will split this file into sub-agent assignments.

**Project:** FamilyBizness, a single-family-office (SFO) prototype built for the Hult ENT family business coursework.

**Written without inspecting the repository.** All file names, component names and data paths below are unknown. Every agent must discover the real ones with search before editing anything.



How to use this file: read "Shared standards" and "Naming model" first, because every workstream depends on them. Then pick up workstreams according to the dependency table. Each task has an ID so reports can reference it.



---



## 0. Shared standards (read before touching any UI)



These rules exist because the sidebar, floating dock and settings pages keep coming back with padding and margin mistakes. Treat them as binding for every workstream.



### 0.1 Padding and margin are different tools



| Property | What it is | Use it for |

|---|---|---|

| Padding | Space inside an element, between its border and its content. The element's background and hit area extend through it. | Breathing room inside components: nav items, cards, panels, buttons, inputs. Indentation of nested nav items. |

| Margin | Space outside an element, separating it from siblings. Transparent, never clickable. | Almost nothing. Separate siblings with `gap` on the flex or grid parent instead. |



Rules that follow from this:



1. **Indent nested nav items with `padding-left`, never `margin-left`.** Padding keeps the hover and active background spanning the full row width, so the indent shows as a shifted label inside an aligned row. Margin shifts the whole row and produces the deformed indents currently visible on Stewardship and Calendar.

2. **Space siblings with `gap` on the container.** Do not put `margin-top` on children. Child margins stack, collapse unpredictably and leak out of containers, which is the likely cause of the extra space above the logo and logo name.

3. **No leading or trailing margin at a container edge.** The first and last child of a container must not add space beyond the container's own padding.

4. **One spacing scale.** Define tokens on a 4px base (4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48). No raw pixel values in components.

5. **Fixed icon column.** Every nav row has an icon slot of identical width and height, whether or not the item has an icon, so labels align at every depth. This fixes the inconsistent icon padding in the sidebar.

6. **Indent step is a token.** Each depth adds the same `--nav-indent-step` to the label's `padding-left`. Depth is data, not hand-tuned CSS per item.

7. **Touch targets are at least 44px tall** on coarse pointers, achieved with padding.



Before changing any spacing, the agent must record for each affected element: which property currently creates the space (padding, margin, gap, or an inline style), and which element owns it. Fix the owner, not the symptom.



### 0.2 Navigation and settings semantics (Jakob's Law)



Jakob's Law says users spend most of their time on other products and expect this one to work the same way. For navigation that means following established conventions closely and leaving novelty to the content. Every decision below carries a rationale, and the agent must write those rationales into `docs/design/navigation-and-settings.md` (also feeds the About page documentation, task 29).



1. **One navigation config drives everything.** A single data structure (sections, pages, depth, icon, route, allowed roles) renders the main sidebar, the floating dock, the settings sidebar and the role filtering. Rationale: the dock currently fails to mirror the sidebar because they are separate implementations. One source of truth removes that class of bug.

2. **Left vertical sidebar for sections with sub-pages.** This is the dominant convention for dashboards and settings (GitHub, Stripe, Google account, macOS and Windows settings). Top tab rows are removed for Family Constitution, Portfolio and Scenario, and Settings.

3. **Flat, always-visible sub-pages.** Sub-pages sit indented under their section header. No dropdowns or accordions, because hidden items cost discoverability and the finance dropdown was already rejected.

4. **Grouped by user task, ordered by frequency.** Group labels are plain nouns. Most-used first, destructive or rare actions last.

5. **Current location is always visible.** Active item has a distinct background and weight, and `aria-current="page"`. The parent section header shows as the active ancestor.

6. **Semantic markup.** `nav` landmark with an `aria-label`, nested `ul` and `li`, real links for navigation (not click-handler divs), one `h1` per page, section headings in order, no skipped levels.

7. **Master-detail layout.** Sidebar on the left, content on the right with a stable max width. On narrow screens the sidebar collapses to a list view that drills into the detail page, with a clear back affordance.

8. **Collapse control.** The collapse icon has a dynamic tooltip and accessible name that switches between "Open menu" and "Close menu", and carries `aria-expanded`. In the collapsed rail every icon shows its label as a tooltip.

9. **Every page is deep-linkable.** Each sub-page has its own URL or hash route so refresh, back button and sharing work.

10. **Consistent form rows in settings.** Label, control, helper text, in that order, same spacing everywhere. Settings changes apply immediately with visible confirmation, or have an explicit Save with a dirty-state indicator. Pick one pattern and use it on every settings page.



---



## 1. Naming model



There are two names and they never mix.



| Name | Value | Where it appears |

|---|---|---|

| Internal code name | `familybizness` | GitHub repository name, package name, folder names, commit messages, the Claude project, docs for developers. Never shown in the product UI. |

| Display name | One variable: the family surname | Every user-visible string in the product. |



**N1. Single display variable.** Create one config value, `FAMILY_NAME`, holding the surname. It is read once from localStorage (with a sensible fallback), editable in Settings and on the landing page header at the top of Stewardship, and consumed everywhere through one accessor. Every other display string is derived from it, for example "{FAMILY_NAME} Family Office" for the product title, page titles and SEO metadata. The surname is undecided (candidates mentioned: Jarwal, Slavonki), so ship with a neutral placeholder and make changing it a one-field edit with an immediate, global effect.



**N2. Purge hardcoded names.** Search the whole codebase for "Golden Bird", "Goldenbird", "Project Golden Bird" and "ENT-0301". None may remain in UI strings. Remove or replace with the variable. The donor constitution is a different, already-fed document, and the new SFO is a hypothetical separate family, so the donor family's name must not leak into the interface.



**N3. Sweep for "Ghostfolio".** The name must not appear anywhere in the UI, including X-ray and Risk. Source-level attribution belongs only in the open-source licenses and credits page (task 29).



---



## 2. Roles and visibility



Roles: **Associate**, **Managing Director**, **Family Patriarch**, **Family Board**, **Legal Counsel**. The earlier titles "Associate / Family Technician" and "Managing Director / CEO" are shortened to the plain names.



| Role | Can see |

|---|---|

| Managing Director | Everything |

| Family Patriarch | Everything |

| Associate | Everything except Family Constitution and Goals |

| Family Board | Goals and the Capital and Liquidity overview only. Detailed finance pages hidden. |

| Legal Counsel | Investment screens only. Finance screens hidden. |

**R2.** Where the Family Board and Legal Counsel boundary falls exactly (which finance pages count as "overview" versus "detail", which pages count as "investment screens") is for the agent to decide from the final sidebar, and to record in the docs.

**R3.** Profile switcher: on short vertical breakpoints (mobile and tablet), the profile picker at the top becomes a swipeable slide carousel. Build it after touch handling is fixed (W1-6).

## 3. Target information architecture



Final names are the agent's to refine. The brief is to replace generic labels with terms a real single family office uses. "Family Business" as a header in a wealth platform does not fit and must be renamed. Structure below is binding, labels are placeholders.

**Project:** FamilyBizness, a single-family-office (SFO) prototype built for the Hult ENT family business coursework.

**Written without inspecting the repository.** All file names, component names and data paths below are unknown. Every agent must discover the real ones with search before editing anything.



How to use this file: read "Shared standards" and "Naming model" first, because every workstream depends on them. Then pick up workstreams according to the dependency table. Each task has an ID so reports can reference it.



---



## 0. Shared standards (read before touching any UI)



These rules exist because the sidebar, floating dock and settings pages keep coming back with padding and margin mistakes. Treat them as binding for every workstream.



### 0.1 Padding and margin are different tools



| Property | What it is | Use it for |

|---|---|---|

| Padding | Space inside an element, between its border and its content. The element's background and hit area extend through it. | Breathing room inside components: nav items, cards, panels, buttons, inputs. Indentation of nested nav items. |

| Margin | Space outside an element, separating it from siblings. Transparent, never clickable. | Almost nothing. Separate siblings with `gap` on the flex or grid parent instead. |



Rules that follow from this:



1. **Indent nested nav items with `padding-left`, never `margin-left`.** Padding keeps the hover and active background spanning the full row width, so the indent shows as a shifted label inside an aligned row. Margin shifts the whole row and produces the deformed indents currently visible on Stewardship and Calendar.

2. **Space siblings with `gap` on the container.** Do not put `margin-top` on children. Child margins stack, collapse unpredictably and leak out of containers, which is the likely cause of the extra space above the logo and logo name.

3. **No leading or trailing margin at a container edge.** The first and last child of a container must not add space beyond the container's own padding.

4. **One spacing scale.** Define tokens on a 4px base (4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48). No raw pixel values in components.

5. **Fixed icon column.** Every nav row has an icon slot of identical width and height, whether or not the item has an icon, so labels align at every depth. This fixes the inconsistent icon padding in the sidebar.

6. **Indent step is a token.** Each depth adds the same `--nav-indent-step` to the label's `padding-left`. Depth is data, not hand-tuned CSS per item.

7. **Touch targets are at least 44px tall** on coarse pointers, achieved with padding.



Before changing any spacing, the agent must record for each affected element: which property currently creates the space (padding, margin, gap, or an inline style), and which element owns it. Fix the owner, not the symptom.



### 0.2 Navigation and settings semantics (Jakob's Law)



Jakob's Law says users spend most of their time on other products and expect this one to work the same way. For navigation that means following established conventions closely and leaving novelty to the content. Every decision below carries a rationale, and the agent must write those rationales into `docs/design/navigation-and-settings.md` (also feeds the About page documentation, task 29).



1. **One navigation config drives everything.** A single data structure (sections, pages, depth, icon, route, allowed roles) renders the main sidebar, the floating dock, the settings sidebar and the role filtering. Rationale: the dock currently fails to mirror the sidebar because they are separate implementations. One source of truth removes that class of bug.

2. **Left vertical sidebar for sections with sub-pages.** This is the dominant convention for dashboards and settings (GitHub, Stripe, Google account, macOS and Windows settings). Top tab rows are removed for Family Constitution, Portfolio and Scenario, and Settings.

3. **Flat, always-visible sub-pages.** Sub-pages sit indented under their section header. No dropdowns or accordions, because hidden items cost discoverability and the finance dropdown was already rejected.

4. **Grouped by user task, ordered by frequency.** Group labels are plain nouns. Most-used first, destructive or rare actions last.

5. **Current location is always visible.** Active item has a distinct background and weight, and `aria-current="page"`. The parent section header shows as the active ancestor.

6. **Semantic markup.** `nav` landmark with an `aria-label`, nested `ul` and `li`, real links for navigation (not click-handler divs), one `h1` per page, section headings in order, no skipped levels.

7. **Master-detail layout.** Sidebar on the left, content on the right with a stable max width. On narrow screens the sidebar collapses to a list view that drills into the detail page, with a clear back affordance.

8. **Collapse control.** The collapse icon has a dynamic tooltip and accessible name that switches between "Open menu" and "Close menu", and carries `aria-expanded`. In the collapsed rail every icon shows its label as a tooltip.

9. **Every page is deep-linkable.** Each sub-page has its own URL or hash route so refresh, back button and sharing work.

10. **Consistent form rows in settings.** Label, control, helper text, in that order, same spacing everywhere. Settings changes apply immediately with visible confirmation, or have an explicit Save with a dirty-state indicator. Pick one pattern and use it on every settings page.



---



## 1. Naming model



There are two names and they never mix.



| Name | Value | Where it appears |

|---|---|---|

| Internal code name | `familybizness` | GitHub repository name, package name, folder names, commit messages, the Claude project, docs for developers. Never shown in the product UI. |

| Display name | One variable: the family surname | Every user-visible string in the product. |



**N1. Single display variable.** Create one config value, `FAMILY_NAME`, holding the surname. It is read once from localStorage (with a sensible fallback), editable in Settings and on the landing page header at the top of Stewardship, and consumed everywhere through one accessor. Every other display string is derived from it, for example "{FAMILY_NAME} Family Office" for the product title, page titles and SEO metadata. The surname is undecided (candidates mentioned: Jarwal, Slavonki), so ship with a neutral placeholder and make changing it a one-field edit with an immediate, global effect.



**N2. Purge hardcoded names.** Search the whole codebase for "Golden Bird", "Goldenbird", "Project Golden Bird" and "ENT-0301". None may remain in UI strings. Remove or replace with the variable. The donor constitution is a different, already-fed document, and the new SFO is a hypothetical separate family, so the donor family's name must not leak into the interface.



**N3. Sweep for "Ghostfolio".** The name must not appear anywhere in the UI, including X-ray and Risk. Source-level attribution belongs only in the open-source licenses and credits page (task 29).



---



## 2. Roles and visibility



Roles: **Associate**, **Managing Director**, **Family Patriarch**, **Family Board**, **Legal Counsel**. The earlier titles "Associate / Family Technician" and "Managing Director / CEO" are shortened to the plain names.



| Role | Can see |

|---|---|

| Managing Director | Everything |

| Family Patriarch | Everything |

| Associate | Everything except Family Constitution and Goals |

| Family Board | Goals and the Capital and Liquidity overview only. Detailed finance pages hidden. |

| Legal Counsel | Investment screens only. Finance screens hidden. |



**R1.** Implement as an allowed-roles field in the navigation config (see 0.2 item 1). Filtering must apply to the main sidebar, floating dock, Ctrl+K search results and direct URL access. A hidden page must not be reachable by typing its route.

**R2.** Where the Family Board and Legal Counsel boundary falls exactly (which finance pages count as "overview" versus "detail", which pages count as "investment screens") is for the agent to decide from the final sidebar, and to record in the docs.

**R3.** Profile switcher: on short vertical breakpoints (mobile and tablet), the profile picker at the top becomes a swipeable slide carousel. Build it after touch handling is fixed (W1-6).



---



## 3. Target information architecture



Final names are the agent's to refine. The brief is to replace generic labels with terms a real single family office uses. "Family Business" as a header in a wealth platform does not fit and must be renamed. Structure below is binding, labels are placeholders.



1. **Stewardship** (homepage and landing page, holds the `FAMILY_NAME` field)

2. **Family Constitution** (H3 under the title: "Live connected to data lake")

   - Charter, with revisions and adoption

   - Investment Policy Statement (includes the art and culture text, see Appendix A)

   - Governance and Succession

   - Estate Planning

3. **Day-to-day Operations**

   - Calendar

   - Family Goals (moved here from Governance, includes the goals manager)

4. **Capital and Liquidity**

   - Overview (expanded liquidity-event detail)

   - Budget and Expenditure (merged with Spending Insights)

   - Investment Fees

   - Trading Analytics

   - Financial Statements (own page, accounting-software connection banner)

5. **Portfolio and Scenario** (all panes in the left sidebar, no top tabs)

   - Includes Stochastic Process Lab and Scenario Analysis as highlighted pages

   - FIRE, X-ray and Risk, and the rest of the existing panes

6. **Settings** (own left sidebar, see workstream W2-S)

7. **About** (own page)



---



## 4. Workstreams and dependencies



| ID | Workstream | Depends on | Can run in parallel with |

|---|---|---|---|

| W0 | Spacing tokens and shared standards (section 0) | none | Nothing else should start UI work before the tokens exist |

| W1 | Data correctness: goals, spending, search, touch | none | W0 |

| W2 | Navigation, IA, dock, sidebar polish, name variable | W0 | W1, W4 |

| W2-S | Settings sidebar and settings pages | W0, W2 nav config | W4, W5 |

| W3 | Role-based views and profile carousel | W1-6 (touch), W2 nav config | W4, W5 |

| W4 | Content and data population | W2 (page homes exist) | W5, W6 |

| W5 | Portfolio and Scenario analytics | W2 (pages exist) | W4, W6 |

| W6 | About, credits, licenses, documentation | W2, N3 | W4, W5 |

| W7 | Local CI/CD, assets, docs, prompts log | W2 to W6 mostly done | UI polish agent (W8) |

| W8 | Remaining UI/UX breakage sweep | W0 | Runs alongside everything from W0 onward |

| W9 | Local commit | Everything | Nothing |



**Conflict warning.** The navigation config and the spacing tokens are shared files. Only W0 owns the tokens and only W2 owns the nav config. Other agents request changes through those owners or make small additive edits and report them.



---



## 5. Workstream detail



### W0. Spacing tokens



- **W0-1.** Add the spacing scale, `--nav-indent-step`, icon column size and touch-target tokens (0.1).

- **W0-2.** Audit existing components for margin used as padding or as indentation, and list offenders in `docs/design/spacing-audit.md`.



### W1. Data correctness



- **W1-1. Single source of truth for goals, expenditure and spending tracking.** The three currently disagree. Make one store that all three read from, and wire each connection explicitly. Verify by tracing every consumer by hand, then add a test that changes a goal and asserts expenditure and spending views update.

- **W1-2. "Short of" bug.** Updating a specific goal always shows the goal as short of its target. Find the cause in the update path and fix it.

- **W1-3. Stale goal UI.** Goal views sometimes do not refresh after the data changes. Fix subscription or state propagation.

- **W1-4. Goals manager.** The specific goal-tracking feature from the add-ons is missing. Search the donor and add-on code, restore it, and wire it to the W1-1 store. It lives under Day-to-day Operations, Family Goals.

- **W1-5. Ctrl+K search.** Fix it, make it respect role visibility (R1) once that exists, and index the new page names.

- **W1-6. Touch gestures and sensitivity.** Gesture support is broken. Fix it before W3's carousel.



### W2. Navigation and information architecture



- **W2-1.** Build the single navigation config (0.2 item 1) and render the sidebar, floating dock and settings sidebar from it.

- **W2-2.** Move every top pane or tab row to the left sidebar for Family Constitution, Portfolio and Scenario, and Settings.

- **W2-3.** Finance sub-pages become flat, indented pages with identical tokens. Remove the dropdown.

- **W2-4.** Apply the target IA in section 3 and replace generic taxonomy with real SFO terminology.

- **W2-5.** Fix the floating dock so menus translate from the sidebar structure exactly.

- **W2-6.** Sidebar polish: fix deformed indents on Stewardship and Calendar (0.1 rule 1), consistent icon padding (0.1 rule 5), remove the extra space above the logo and logo name (0.1 rule 2), dynamic "Open menu" and "Close menu" tooltips on the collapse icon (0.2 item 8).

- **W2-7.** Implement N1 and N2.



### W2-S. Settings (high priority, historically mishandled)



Settings has repeatedly come out wrong because agents treated padding and margin as interchangeable and skipped the convention logic behind settings navigation. Build it against section 0 and the points below.



- **S-1.** Replace the top navigation with a **settings sidebar** on the left, rendered from the nav config.

- **S-2.** Proposed groups (the agent may rename, but each group needs a written rationale): General (family name, profile and role), Appearance (theme, density, motion), Data (data lake connection, import and export, reset local storage), Integrations (accounting software connection), Accessibility. About stays its own page.

- **S-3.** The family name field (N1) lives under General and shows a live preview of how it appears in the product title.

- **S-4.** Layout: sidebar fixed width, content column with a stable max width, page `h1`, then sections with `h2`, then form rows (0.2 item 10). Spacing between sections uses `gap`, internal space uses padding.

- **S-5.** Narrow screens: sidebar becomes a list that drills into the detail page with a back control.

- **S-6.** Each settings page is deep-linkable, keyboard navigable and exposes `aria-current` on the active entry.

- **S-7.** Write `docs/design/navigation-and-settings.md` listing, for every settings group and every layout decision, the convention it follows and why. An agent finishing S-1 to S-6 without this document has not finished.

- **S-8.** Verification: screenshot every settings page at desktop, tablet and mobile widths and compare row alignment, indent and padding against the tokens. Report any element where spacing comes from margin.



### W3. Roles



- **W3-1.** Implement section 2 (R1, R2).

- **W3-2.** Profile carousel (R3) on short vertical breakpoints, after W1-6.



### W4. Content and data



- **W4-1. Calendar.** Heavily populate it and fill December and January as well. It currently looks too empty. Use realistic SFO events: investment committee meetings, trustee and board meetings, tax and filing deadlines, insurance renewals, distributions, family assemblies, philanthropy decisions, education milestones.

- **W4-2. Cash gifts.** The page that holds it is unknown to the author. Search the codebase (case-insensitive: "cash gift", "cashGift", "gifts") to locate it, populate the empty data, and report where it lives so the IA can be confirmed.

- **W4-3. Budget and Expenditure.** Merge with Spending Insights into one page, wired to the W1-1 store.

- **W4-4. Capital and Liquidity overview.** Expand how liquidity events unfold: sources, timing, triggers, reserve levels, sequencing.

- **W4-5. Financial Statements page.**

  - Own page under Capital and Liquidity.

  - H2 stating the page will connect to QuickBooks or equivalent. Research competitor names and list them (candidates: Xero, Sage, NetSuite, Zoho Books, FreshBooks), verifying current names before writing them.

  - Expand statement details and financial inputs.

  - Present as a clearly marked planned integration. Do not claim a working connection.

- **W4-6. IPS panel.**

  - Improve the natural language processing section.

  - Render all sections as open divs, no dropdowns.

  - Add the art and culture text (Appendix A).

  - Use the `FAMILY_NAME` variable, never the donor family name.

- **W4-7.** Remove the "Populate from your selected assignment material" element.

- **W4-8.** Tighten the category definitions so they match what real single-family offices use (asset classes, entity types, expense categories).



### W5. Portfolio and Scenario analytics



- **W5-1.** Stochastic Process Lab and Scenario Analysis each become a highlighted page in the Portfolio and Scenario sidebar section.

- **W5-2. Seven models.** Run on the static data and the updated financial inputs, and graph results. Two exist today, so the total target is seven. Suggested candidates, agent may substitute: geometric Brownian motion Monte Carlo, Ornstein-Uhlenbeck mean reversion, Heston stochastic volatility, Merton jump diffusion, Markov regime switching, GARCH(1,1) volatility forecast, bootstrapped historical simulation, and a small neural-network or LSTM projection. Pick seven in total.

- **W5-3.** Static output only. Use seeded random number generators so results are reproducible, and either precompute to JSON at build time or run client-side. No server calls.

- **W5-4. FIRE page.** Fix vertical and horizontal alignment using the section 0 rules.

- **W5-5. X-ray and Risk.** Surface the elements currently hidden behind model views. Replace the Methods category, which holds only definitions, with small KPI cards, each with a header and a calculated numeric value.

- **W5-6.** Confirm N3: no "Ghostfolio" anywhere in the UI.



### W6. About, credits, documentation



- **W6-1.** About page links directly to the repository.

- **W6-2.** Credits: GitHub repository credits, image credits (the author will add image credits later, so provide a structured place for them), open-source licenses and attribution including the donor code and any component derived from it.

- **W6-3.** Deepen documentation across the project. The donor code must be documented in particular: what was taken, from where, under what license, and what was changed.



### W7. Local CI/CD, assets, docs, prompts



**Local CI/CD (high priority, cost-driven).** The author has been charged too much for GitHub Actions. All automation must run on this machine and nothing may run on GitHub.



- **W7-1. Disable GitHub Actions.** Audit `.github/workflows`. Remove or neutralize every workflow so no push triggers a hosted run. Do not add new workflows there. If workflows are kept for reference, move them to the CI folder as inert examples.

- **W7-2. One local CI folder**, for example `ci/`, containing every script. Provide a single entry command that runs, in order: install, lint, typecheck, tests, static build, link check, and asset check. Provide it as an npm script and as a PowerShell script, because the main shell is PowerShell on Windows.

- **W7-3. Static output.** The build produces a plain static directory (for example `dist/`) with relative asset paths and no server runtime, deployable as-is to GitLab Pages, Cloudflare Pages or any static host. Document that in `ci/README.md`.

- **W7-4. Inert deploy examples.** Provide `ci/examples/gitlab-ci.yml.example` and Cloudflare Pages settings notes. They must not live at paths that auto-run on any platform.

- **W7-5. Live preview command.** A single command that builds or serves the static output locally.

- **W7-6. Billing note.** The GitHub billing warning in the UI may be a bug. Commit and push work from the attached PowerShell, so it is not expected to block anything. If it does block, report it in the final summary and carry on.



**Assets.**



- **W7-7.** Create an `assets/` folder tree with placeholder images for every slot the product and SEO metadata use.

- **W7-8.** Add a sidecar `assets/README.md` with a table per slot: file path, purpose, aspect ratio, recommended pixel size, format (PNG, JPG, SVG, GIF), and whether transparency is required. GIF is supported. Suggested starting rows: Open Graph image (1.91:1, 1200 by 630), favicon set (1:1), logo (transparent PNG or SVG), hero image (16:9). The agent adds the rest by scanning the UI.

- **W7-9.** Reference those placeholder paths in the SEO metadata (Open Graph, Twitter card, favicons, manifest).



**Docs and metadata.**



- **W7-10.** Prepare local docs, `README.md`, GitHub repository metadata (description, topics, homepage) as a file, and SEO metadata. The README uses `familybizness` as the code name and the display variable for product-facing text.



**AI prompts log.**



- **W7-11.** Produce one markdown file, `docs/AI_PROMPTS_LOG.md`, containing every prompt the author sent, in append-style format so it reads as one long log.

- **W7-12.** Sources: `.cursor`, `.claude` (project transcripts), and any other agent history found on this machine, including Codex chat history and workflow logs. Search both the repository and the user profile directories (on Windows, under `%USERPROFILE%`).

- **W7-13.** Include only user prompts, in chronological order, each tagged with source tool and timestamp when available. Do not invent entries. Scan for secrets such as API keys and tokens and redact them.

- **W7-14.** Provide an idempotent append script in `ci/` so rerunning it adds only new prompts to the end of the file.

- **W7-15.** No handover zip is wanted. The submission is the repository link plus a separate markdown report.



### W8. Remaining UI/UX breakage



A dedicated sub-agent sweeps the smaller visual bugs that are not blockers. It works from the W0 tokens, screenshots each page at three widths, fixes spacing and alignment defects, and reports a list of what changed. It must not edit the nav config or tokens directly.



### W9. Local commit (last)



- **W9-1.** Only after the local CI entry command passes.

- **W9-2.** Create a local commit. Do not push. The author will push from PowerShell.

- **W9-3.** Use a conventional commit message that summarizes the phases, and make sure generated build output and secrets are not staged.



---



## 6. Definition of done



1. `FAMILY_NAME` change in Settings updates every visible product string, and no instance of the donor family's name or "Ghostfolio" remains in the UI.

2. Goals, expenditure and spending views agree after any single edit.

3. Each role sees exactly the pages in section 2, including via Ctrl+K and direct URL.

4. Sidebar, floating dock and settings sidebar render from one config and match.

5. No indent uses margin, and no stray space sits above the logo.

6. `docs/design/navigation-and-settings.md` exists and justifies each navigation and settings decision.

7. The local CI command passes and the static output deploys unchanged to a static host.

8. No GitHub Actions workflow remains active.

9. `docs/AI_PROMPTS_LOG.md`, `assets/README.md` and the README exist.

10. One local commit exists, unpushed.



---



## Appendix A: Art and culture text for the Investment Policy Statement



> A family business expenditure and philanthropy may commission art, employ local makers, develop event formats and support a publishing or media venture. Each activity creates a different claim on capital. The hotel can buy decoration as an operating expense; a collection vehicle can acquire a documented work as an investment; a foundation can fund artists without claiming a financial return. Shared family taste is insufficient to classify a transaction.

>

> The family shall create and support culture as well as own financial assets. Painting, sculpture, visual arts, film, music, design, publishing, performance, digital production and architecture are eligible activities.

>

> **Art as an asset.** Art is evaluated with the discipline appropriate to a costly, heterogeneous and often illiquid asset.

**R2.** Where the Family Board and Legal Counsel boundary falls exactly (which finance pages count as "overview" versus "detail", which pages count as "investment screens") is for the agent to decide from the final sidebar, and to record in the docs.

**R3.** Profile switcher: on short vertical breakpoints (mobile and tablet), the profile picker at the top becomes a swipeable slide carousel. Build it after touch handling is fixed (W1-6).



---



## 3. Target information architecture



Final names are the agent's to refine. The brief is to replace generic labels with terms a real single family office uses. "Family Business" as a header in a wealth platform does not fit and must be renamed. Structure below is binding, labels are placeholders.



1. **Stewardship** (homepage and landing page, holds the `FAMILY_NAME` field)

2. **Family Constitution** (H3 under the title: "Live connected to data lake")

4. **Capital and Liquidity**

   - Investment Fees

   - Trading Analytics

   - Financial Statements (own page, accounting-software connection banner)

5. **Portfolio and Scenario** (all panes in the left sidebar, no top tabs)

   - Includes Stochastic Process Lab and Scenario Analysis as highlighted pages

   - FIRE, X-ray and Risk, and the rest of the existing panes

6. **Settings** (own left sidebar, see workstream W2-S)

7. **About** (own page)

|---|---|---|---|

- **W7-12.** Sources: `.cursor`, `.claude` (project transcripts), and any other agent history found on this machine, including Codex chat history and workflow logs. Search both the repository and the user profile directories (on Windows, under `%USERPROFILE%`).

- **W7-14.** Provide an idempotent append script in `ci/` so rerunning it adds only new prompts to the end of the file.

- **W7-15.** No handover zip is wanted. The submission is the repository link plus a separate markdown report.



### W8. Remaining UI/UX breakage



A dedicated sub-agent sweeps the smaller visual bugs that are not blockers. It works from the W0 tokens, screenshots each page at three widths, fixes spacing and alignment defects, and reports a list of what changed. It must not edit the nav config or tokens directly.



### W9. Local commit (last)



- **W9-1.** Only after the local CI entry command passes.

- **W9-2.** Create a local commit. Do not push. The author will push from PowerShell.

- **W9-3.** Use a conventional commit message that summarizes the phases, and make sure generated build output and secrets are not staged.

8. No GitHub Actions workflow remains active.

9. `docs/AI_PROMPTS_LOG.md`, `assets/README.md` and the README exist.

10. One local commit exists, unpushed.



---



## Appendix A: Art and culture text for the Investment Policy Statement



> A family business expenditure and philanthropy may commission art, employ local makers, develop event formats and support a publishing or media venture. Each activity creates a different claim on capital. The hotel can buy decoration as an operating expense; a collection vehicle can acquire a documented work as an investment; a foundation can fund artists without claiming a financial return. Shared family taste is insufficient to classify a transaction.

>

> The family shall create and support culture as well as own financial assets. Painting, sculpture, visual arts, film, music, design, publishing, performance, digital production and architecture are eligible activities.

>

> **Art as an asset.** Art is evaluated with the discipline appropriate to a costly, heterogeneous and often illiquid asset.

## 2026-11-06T15:29:48.844Z — T3

# Using Reicon with Vanilla JS & CDN



The official vanilla JavaScript package and CDN web components for Reicon. This package allows you to easily add precise, vector-based SVG icons to any JavaScript project or web application without framework dependencies.



## What you can accomplish

- Import individual icons as DOM element factories in vanilla JS

- Fetch SVG markup as strings directly (fully SSR/Node.js compatible)

- Register and render custom elements (`<re-icon>`) inside HTML templates or SPAs

- Load all icons via CDN script tags without build steps or bundlers

- Tree-shake unused icons automatically when using modern bundlers



---



## 1. Vanilla JS / Bundler (NPM)



Install the package using your preferred package manager and import tree-shakeable icons directly in your application code.



### Installation

```bash

npm install reicon

# or

yarn add reicon

# or

pnpm add reicon

```



### Creating DOM Elements

Import named icons directly from `reicon`. Each icon is a factory function that returns a native `SVGSVGElement`.

```javascript

import { Home, ShieldCheck } from 'reicon';



// Create SVG elements

const home = Home({ size: 24 });

const shield = ShieldCheck({ size: 32, color: '#9B8AFB', weight: 'Filled' });



// Append directly to document

document.body.appendChild(home);

document.body.appendChild(shield);

```



### Server-Side Rendering (SSR) & SVG Strings

To render icons on the server (SSR, Node.js, or framework environments), use the `toSvg()` method on the icon functions. This returns raw SVG strings without using DOM APIs.

```javascript

import { Home } from 'reicon';



// Get raw SVG string - works on server side!

const svgString = Home.toSvg({ size: 24, color: 'currentColor' });



// Inject into HTML output

res.send(`<div class="icon-wrap">${svgString}</div>`);

```



### Registering Custom Element (`<re-icon>`)

You can register and import the web component runtime right from your npm installation. Simply import `reicon/element` once in your application entry point.

```javascript

import 'reicon/element';



// Now use <re-icon icon="home"></re-icon> in your HTML templates!

```



### Direct Import for Smallest Bundles

For optimal build performance, import individual icons directly from their path:

```javascript

import Home from 'reicon/icons/Home';

const homeSvg = Home({ size: 24 });

```



---



## 2. CDN & HTML (No Build Tools)



Perfect for static HTML websites, legacy applications, and prototyping. Include a script tag and render icons instantly.



### Register Custom Element (`<re-icon>`)

Include the script tag inside your HTML page. This registers a reactive `<re-icon>` component that supports dynamic styling, sizes, weights, and gradients.

```html

<script src="https://unpkg.com/reicon/cdn/reicon.js"></script>

```

*Or load a specific version:* `https://unpkg.com/reicon@latest/cdn/reicon.js`



### Basic CDN Usage

Simply add the `<re-icon>` tags directly in your HTML:

```html

<re-icon icon="home"></re-icon>

<re-icon icon="shield-check" weight="filled" size="32" color="#9B8AFB"></re-icon>

```



### Load Functions globally via Script tag

If you want to use the global `reicon` object functions directly in a browser script tag:

```html

<script src="https://unpkg.com/reicon@latest/umd/reicon.js"></script>

<script>

  // Create icon elements via global object

  document.body.appendChild(reicon.Home({ size: 32 }));

</script>

```



### Customizing Elements (Attributes)

You can customize `<re-icon>` elements using reactive HTML attributes. Updates will be rendered instantly.

```html

<!-- Size -->

<re-icon icon="home" size="16"></re-icon>

<re-icon icon="home" size="32"></re-icon>



<!-- Color -->

<re-icon icon="heart" color="#ef4444"></re-icon>

<re-icon icon="heart" color="rgb(99, 102, 241)"></re-icon>



<!-- Weight -->

<re-icon icon="star" weight="outline"></re-icon>

<re-icon icon="star" weight="filled"></re-icon>

```



### Styling with CSS

The `<re-icon>` element acts like an inline block. It automatically inherits its parent's text color, allowing CSS utility-classes to adjust color naturally.

```html

<style>

  .icon-primary {

    color: #9B8AFB;

  }

</style>



<re-icon icon="home" class="icon-primary"></re-icon>



<!-- Inherits color from parent -->

<div style="color: #ef4444;">

  <re-icon icon="heart"></re-icon>

</div>

```



### Full Example HTML Page

A complete HTML document importing Reicon via CDN and showcasing customizations:

```html

<!DOCTYPE html>

<html lang="en">

<head>

  <meta charset="UTF-8">

  <title>Reicon Page</title>

  <script src="https://unpkg.com/reicon@latest/cdn/reicon.js"></script>

</head>

<body>

  <nav>

    <re-icon icon="home" size="20"></re-icon>

    <re-icon icon="user" size="20"></re-icon>

  </nav>

  <main>

    <h1>

      <re-icon icon="shield-check" size="28" weight="filled" color="#9B8AFB"></re-icon>

      App Verified

    </h1>

  </main>

</body>

</html>

```



> **Note:** If you are compiling your project with modern bundlers (e.g. Vite, Webpack, rollup), prefer installing via `npm install reicon` to enjoy full tree-shaking, static typing, and faster loading speeds.



---
---
---
---
---
---
---
---
--

# Recovered Planning History
